What reaches a human after 65 cases

A 49-case synthetic benchmark, not live Ryde volume. Each count is recomputed from the stored rulings.

Cases in the batch
65

Route deviation, no-show fee and safety scenarios, each with reference outcomes derived from the written policy.

→
Core decisions auto-cleared
46/ 65

28 auto-cleared for voucher issuance ($150.23 in Ryde vouchers) and 18 with no refund due. Each one passed the pre-execution policy check.

→
Sent to a person
19

Each one was stopped by the policy check or escalated by the judge. They arrive with the facts, both arguments and the policy trace already prepared.

Where it plugs in

Inputs

What the court reads

trip · GPS · chat · fare · profiles · OneMap route · LTA traffic · weather

Outputs

What it hands back

cleared / needs a person · proposed actions · plain-language notices · audit receipt

Production connectors (not included)

Where Ryde would connect

  • trip records in → one case record
  • refund → wallet / voucher API
  • account flag → trust and safety queue
  • appeals → audit receipt

31.9 sReplay
Route deviation · B-0002

Detour · consent given beforehand

30.4 s
Dispute statement

The driver took a longer way round than the app showed and I paid more than the estimate. I want the difference back.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Clarke Quay → Bedok Mall, estimate 15.5 km / 21.8 min, pickup 12:14 drop-off 12:46; charged $41.09 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Human review

Escalate to a Ryde reviewer: determine which road the 12:29–12:39 segment was actually driven on and whether the rider's 12:27 "Sure, go ahead." (F9) to the driver's named-road proposal (F8) covers it; if it does not, refund 3.27 km × $1.4098/km = $4.61 (RD-2). No account flag (RD-6 not met).

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 19.4 km vs optimal route 15.5 km: +3.9 km (+25%).
  • F2 Trip duration 32.2 min vs estimated 21.8 min (+10.4 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 12:29–12:39: 6.34 km driven where the optimal route covers 3.07 km, adding 3.27 km (up to 755 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $5.54 (3.93 km × $1.4098/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F6 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F7 Total charged $41.09 SGD: base $3.90, distance $27.35 (19.40 km), time $8.26 (31.8 min), waiting $0.11, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F8 Chat #1 12:26 driver: “Traffic ahead on Pan Island Expressway is building up, ok if I go round via Sims Avenue?”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • This RydeTAXI trip ran 19.4 km against an optimal 15.5 km (+3.9 km, +25%) and 32.2 min against a 21.8 min estimate, so the meter billed above the shortest-and-most-direct route.
  • There is exactly one off-route segment: 12:29–12:39, 6.34 km driven where the optimal route covers 3.07 km, adding 3.27 km and straying up to 755 m from the optimal route; no exemption is established for it.
Driver's advocate
  • The trip was a RydeTAXI metered ride (total charged $41.09 with a per-km distance component), so any refund must be computed under the RydeTAXI branch of RD-2, i.e. mileage units only, not the whole fare.
  • There is exactly one off-route segment, 12:29–12:39, which added 3.27 km (6.34 km driven where the optimal route covers 3.07 km); the remaining ~0.66 km of the total +3.9 km excess is corridor wobble/matching noise and is not refundable under RD-2.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 12:29:55 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F8: Chat #1 12:26 driver: “Traffic ahead on Pan Island Expressway is building up, ok if I go round via Sims Avenue?”; F9: Chat #2 12:27 rider: “Sure, go ahead.”; F11: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “Traffic was building up ahead so I asked the rider before turning off via Sims Avenue and the rider agreed.” (F8, F9, F11)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F8, F9)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F17)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4098/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why automation stopped

segment finding undetermined for F3

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the record does not settle whether the rider's agreement covers this segment; a person decides whether $4.61 is refunded.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.
Notices
Notice to the rider
Your dispute on trip B-0002 has been passed to a Ryde reviewer. No decision has been made yet: nothing has been refunded or charged as part of this review. The reviewer will look at what was agreed about the route and which stretch it covered. Ryde will contact you once the review is complete.
Notice to the driver
The dispute on trip B-0002 has been passed to a Ryde reviewer. No decision has been made yet, and nothing changes on your account or earnings as part of this review. The reviewer will look at what was agreed about the route and which stretch it covered. Ryde will contact you once the review is complete.

Route deviation · B-0013

Two detours · first one agreed

36.0 s
Dispute statement

I agreed to go round the jam near the start, but later he went off the route a second time without asking. I want that part refunded.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Woodlands Civic Centre → Orchard Road, estimate 20.6 km / 28.8 min, pickup 18:31 drop-off 19:18; charged $61.32 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Human review

Send to a human reviewer: confirm whether the 18:43 rider message (a reply to the driver's named-road proposal) covers the 18:46-18:56 segment, then apply RD-2 for any segment held unexempted.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 31.0 km vs optimal route 20.6 km: +10.4 km (+50%).
  • F2 Trip duration 47.3 min vs estimated 28.8 min (+18.5 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 18:46–18:56: 7.10 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.43 km, adding 5.66 km (up to 1923 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Off-route segment 19:03–19:12: 5.77 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.00 km, adding 4.78 km (up to 1680 m from the optimal route).
  • F5 Excess distance billed $14.64 (10.38 km × $1.4100/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F6 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F7 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F8 Total charged $61.32 SGD: base $3.90, distance $43.64 (30.95 km), time $12.20 (46.9 min), waiting $0.11, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The trip was metered RydeTAXI, so extra kilometres driven enter the fare directly: actual 31.0 km versus an optimal 20.6 km (+10.4 km, +50%), billed as $14.64 excess distance (10.38 km × $1.4100/km).
  • The GPS track contains two off-route segments: 18:46–18:56 added 5.66 km and 19:03–19:12 added 4.78 km, each therefore a separate segment requiring its own RD-3/RD-4/RD-5 justification.
Driver's advocate
  • Only two off-route segments exist on this trip: 18:46–18:56 (+5.66 km) and 19:03–19:12 (+4.78 km); the complaint concerns the second one, so liability must be assessed segment by segment.
  • The rider personally, before the first segment started at 18:46, sent the message 'Sure, your call.' in reply to the driver's 18:42 proposal 'ok if I go round via Rifle Range Road?' - a message sent before the segment began, and a general route authorisation to take the faster route round the jam.

The second turn-off was my GPS rerouting.” (F9, F10, F12)

Why automation stopped

segment finding undetermined for F3

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the record does not settle whether the rider's agreement covers this segment; a person decides whether $7.98 is refunded.
  • F4: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so $6.74 is refunded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.

Route deviation · B-0023

Detour · consent beforehand, driver has history

30.6 s
Dispute statement

The driver took a longer way round than the app showed and I paid more than the estimate. I want the difference back.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI NEX Serangoon → Suntec City, estimate 11.0 km / 20.7 min, pickup 22:41 drop-off 23:10; charged $32.28 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Human review

Send file to a human reviewer: confirm from the dispatched route/GPS breadcrumb whether the 22:54–23:03 off-route segment was actually driven on Guillemard Road; if it was not (or cannot be confirmed), refund $3.01 (2.13 km × $1.4102/km) as a Ryde voucher and flag the driver's account under RD-6.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 13.6 km vs optimal route 11.0 km: +2.6 km (+24%).
  • F2 Trip duration 29.8 min vs estimated 20.7 min (+9.1 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 22:54–23:03: 3.77 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.64 km, adding 2.13 km (up to 1095 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $3.65 (2.59 km × $1.4102/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F6 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F7 Total charged $32.28 SGD: base $3.90, distance $19.15 (13.58 km), time $7.65 (29.4 min), waiting $0.11, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F8 Chat #1 22:51 driver: “Looks like traffic on Pan Island Expressway is building up, mind if I go round via Guillemard Road?”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS track shows the driver's actual distance was 13.6 km against an optimal 11.0 km (+2.6 km, +24%), and 29.8 min against an estimated 20.7 min (+9.1 min), so the trip was materially longer than the app's route.
  • The track isolates one off-route segment (22:54–23:03) of 3.77 km where the optimal route covers only 1.64 km, adding 2.13 km and reaching up to 1095 m from the optimal route - this is an unexempted detour segment under RD-1, and the ride is a metered RydeTAXI trip so the extra distance flows straight into the fare.
Driver's advocate
  • The trip is RydeTAXI, so the metered fare is billed by distance units and any refund can only be the meter kilometres attributable to an unexcused segment (RD-2). The billed excess distance is $3.65 for 2.59 km at $1.4102/km, and total charged is $32.28 (F4, F7).
  • The only off-route segment on the GPS track runs 22:54–23:03, 3.77 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.64 km, adding 2.13 km (F3); this is a single segment, so at most one segment's extra_km can be considered (RD-1).
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 22:54:50 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount.   • ? Route deviation · B-0050

Detour · "whichever way is faster"

36.3 s
Dispute statement

I told him any faster way was fine, but the meter ended up well above the estimate. I don't think it was faster at all.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Jurong Point → Suntec City, estimate 24.4 km / 33.6 min, pickup 12:50 drop-off 13:38; charged $60.30 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Human review

Issue a voucher refund of $7.09 to the rider (RydeTAXI meter overcharge for the 5.03 km non-exempt off-route segment at $1.4101/km, per NS-7), and remind the driver that a "take whichever way is faster" agreement covers a route to the destination, not a 1391 m excursion off the optimal corridor (RD-1/RD-5).

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 30.0 km vs optimal route 24.4 km: +5.6 km (+23%).
  • F2 Trip duration 48.3 min vs estimated 33.6 min (+14.7 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 13:07–13:19: 7.41 km driven where the optimal route covers 2.38 km, adding 5.03 km (up to 1391 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $7.88 (5.59 km × $1.4101/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F6 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F7 Total charged $60.30 SGD: base $3.90, distance $42.36 (30.04 km), time $12.46 (47.9 min), waiting $0.11, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F8 Chat #1 13:03 driver: “Traffic ahead on Ayer Rajah Expressway is building up, shall I take another way?”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS track shows a single off-route segment 13:07–13:19 in which 7.41 km were driven where the optimal route covers 2.38 km, adding 5.03 km and straying up to 1391 m off the optimal route; this is an off-route segment under RD-1 and, being a metered RydeTAXI trip, the extra kilometres feed straight into the meter.
  • The trip ran 30.0 km against a 24.4 km optimal route (+5.6 km, +23%) and took 48.3 min against a 33.6 min estimate (+14.7 min), so the segment was not a time-saving shortcut and the meter total of $60.30 exceeds what the shortest and most direct route would have produced.
Driver's advocate
  • This is a RydeTAXI metered trip (charged $60.30 with a metered distance/time/waiting breakdown), so any refund is computed under the RydeTAXI branch of RD-2 and the rider's complaint is not evidence of overcharging by itself.
  • The rider gave a general route authorisation before the deviation began: at 13:04 the rider wrote 'Just take whichever way is faster lah, I'm late' (F9), which followed the driver's 13:03 route-change proposal (F8) and therefore precedes the off-route segment that starts at 13:07 (F3); under RD-5 a general 'whichever is faster' authorisation covers the routing chosen to reach the destination.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 13:07:15 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F8: Chat #1 13:03 driver: “Traffic ahead on Ayer Rajah Expressway is building up, shall I take another way?”; F9: Chat #2 13:04 rider: “Just take whichever way is faster lah, I'm late.”; F11: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “Traffic was building up ahead. I asked the rider and the rider said any faster way was fine, so I went round.” (F8, F9, F11)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F8, F9)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F18)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4101/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why automation stopped

not eligible for automation: refund_partial but every segment is exempt → amount would be 0

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found the rider's own message covers this segment, so it is exempt; without that message $7.09 would be refunded.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.

(2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F8: Chat #1 22:35 driver: “Traffic ahead on Pan Island Expressway is building up, ok if I go round via Bukit Timah Road?”; F9: Chat #2 22:36 rider: “Can lah, go ahead.”; F11: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “I asked the rider before turning off via Bukit Timah Road and the rider said can.” (F8, F9, F11)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F8, F9)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F18)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4101/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False

What each side argued
Rider's advocate

Driver's advocate
  • This is a RydeTAXI metered trip (F8: base $3.90, distance $30.54 for 21.66 km, time $8.65, platform $1.47), so under RD-2 any deviation refund is limited to unexempted extra_km × this trip's per-km rate of $1.4100 (F5), i.e. only the metered mileage units - not the whole excess fare and not the waiting time.
  • There are exactly two off-route segments: 17:04–17:11 adding 2.44 km (F3) and 17:18–17:23 adding 2.58 km (F4); RD-1 requires each to be reviewed separately, so only the second can be contested by the rider.

  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 17:04:50 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. We've reviewed this trip and found it partly outside the agreed route.

What decided it: the rider asked for a stop at Clementi Mall and you agreed, so the drive to that stop and the 4-minute stop itself are treated as agreed, and your waiting charge stands. The remaining stretch of driving in that part of the trip ran well beyond the direct route, and nothing in the record ties that extra driving to the agreed stop, so we refunded $2.40 to the rider as a Ryde voucher.

  • F5 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F6 Total charged $24.80 SGD: base $3.90, distance $15.14 (10.74 km), time $4.29 (16.5 min), waiting $0.00, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F7 No in-app chat messages between rider and driver.
  • F8 Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “I went where the GPS sent me; it rerouted. The fare is what the meter showed.”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS track shows one off-route segment on this RydeTAXI trip: 12:12–12:16, where 2.93 km were driven over ground the optimal route covers in 0.80 km, adding 2.13 km and straying up to 954 m from the optimal route (F3); overall the trip ran 10.7 km against an optimal 8.4 km, +2.3 km (+27%) (F1).
  • No exemption applies to that segment: there were no in-app messages at all (F7), so the rider gave no consent of any kind before the segment started, and the driver's post-trip statement that 'the GPS rerouted' me is a claim, not rider consent, and verbal agreement asserted only by the driver does not count (F8).
Driver's advocate
  • The trip was a RydeTAXI metered run: the $24.80 charged is composed of base $3.90, distance $15.14 for 10.74 km, time $4.29 for 16.5 min and platform $1.47, with no waiting fee and no surge (F5, F6), so the complaint is a metered-mileage dispute, not a fixed-quote dispute.
  • The GPS record shows one continuous off-route segment, 12:12–12:16, driving 2.93 km where the optimal route covers 0.80 km and adding 2.13 km (F3), with the whole trip 10.7 km against an 8.4 km optimal route (F1) - that single segment is the only deviation the driver must explain.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 12:12:15 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F8: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “I went where the GPS sent me; it rerouted. The fare is what the meter showed.” (F8)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (no evidence)
  • ✓ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 2 (F14)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4097/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: only if a segment is found non-exempt (RD-6 prior upheld dispute on file)
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance. The account flag is not cleared: it goes to a person in the trust and safety queue.

What would change this ruling

No-show fee · B-0026

No-show · "I'm Here" tapped 600 m away

20.4 s
Dispute statement

The driver marked arrived but was never at the pickup point. I waited there the whole time and then got charged $6.61 as a no-show.

Trip summary

RydeX Bugis Junction → Tanjong Pagar Centre, accepted 12:02:00, cancelled by driver at 12:09:25; cancellation fee $6.61

Proposed outcome

Full refund $6.61 · account flag → a person

Refund the $6.61 no-show cancellation fee to the rider as a Ryde voucher and send the driver's account to Ryde for review of the false "I'm Here" arrival confirmation + flag_account.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Driver's closest approach to pickup: 599 m at 12:06:55. Driver never came within 200 m.
  • F2 Driver stationary 12:05:55–12:09:25 (3 min 30 s) at (1.3048, 103.8549), 600 m from pickup.
  • F3 Accepted 12:02:00. Cancelled by driver at 12:09:25 (7 min 25 s after acceptance). Cancellation fee on record: $6.61.
  • F4 driver_arrived_at is null: the platform never recorded the driver at the pickup point.
  • F5 Driver tapped "I'm Here" at 12:06:15, 600 m from the pickup point at that moment; stayed within 200 m for 0 s after the tap.
  • F6 Cancellation fee charged: $6.61.
  • F7 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F8 Chat #1 12:06 driver: “I'm here already, silver Toyota Altis, please come out”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The driver tapped "I'm Here" at 12:06:15 while he was 600 m from the pickup point, so he was not "at the pick-up location" and NS-1 (ii) fails; the platform itself never recorded him at the pickup point (driver_arrived_at is null).
  • The GPS record shows the driver never came within 200 m of the pickup point - closest approach 599 m at 12:06:55 - and he stayed within 200 m for 0 seconds after the tap, so the continuous-wait requirement of NS-1 (iii) also fails.
Driver's advocate
  • The driver did press "I'm Here" and held position waiting before cancelling: GPS shows the vehicle stationary for 3 min 30 s from 12:05:55 to 12:09:25 (F2), and the "I'm Here" tap was made at 12:06:15 (F5), i.e. the driver then waited at least the statutory 3 minutes at a fixed spot before the 12:09:25 cancellation (F3). This satisfies the waiting-duration element of the no-show test (RD-5, NS-1).
  • The rider herself confirmed she was at the pickup point and could see no car (F9), so the rider's own message supports that the driver was expected at the stated pick-up location and that contact was made; the dispute is therefore about location, not about whether the rider waited, and the driver did not fabricate the wait (F5, F12).
Policy trace
  • ✓ NS-0 who cancelled [case] cancelled_by = driver (F3)
  • ✓ NS-1 GPS trace covers the wait without a hole [case] samples run from the arrival confirmation to the cancellation with no gap beyond the hole threshold (F5)
  • ✓ NS-1 (i) arrival confirmed: driver tapped I'm Here, or the platform auto-confirmed arrival [case] driver_tap at 12:06:15 (F5)
  • ✗ NS-1 (ii) within 200 m of pickup AT THE MOMENT of the arrival confirmation [case] 600 m from pickup at the driver_tap (closest approach overall 599 m) (F5, F1)
  • ✗ NS-1 (iii) stayed at the pickup more than 3 min after the arrival confirmation, until cancelling (NS-1 default: more than 3 min) [case] 0 s continuously inside the radius after the driver_tap at 12:06:15; threshold 180 s (>) (F5)
  • ✓ NS-2 arrival confirmed while not at pickup → flag_account [case] fake arrival confirmation (F5, F1)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_full, escalate; amount = cancellation fee $6.61
  • flag_account required: True
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance. The account flag is not cleared: it goes to a person in the trust and safety queue.

What would change this ruling
  • Had NS-1 (ii) and (iii) both held ((ii) within 200 m of pickup AT THE MOMENT of the arrival confirmation; (iii) stayed at the pickup more than 3 min after the arrival confirmation, until cancelling (NS-1 default: more than 3 min)), the fee would stand and no refund would be due.

What decided it: our GPS records show the vehicle was about 600 metres from your pickup point when the driver tapped "I'm Here," and it never came within the 200 metres we require. The driver also never waited at the pickup after that tap.

Nothing further is needed from you. If you have questions about the voucher, just reply to this message.
Notice to the driver
We reviewed your "I'm Here" arrival confirmation for this trip and are refunding the rider's $6.61 cancellation fee as a Ryde voucher.

What decided it: the GPS record shows your vehicle was about 600 metres from the pickup point when you tapped "I'm Here" and never came within the 200 metres required, with no wait inside that area afterwards. Your written statement about waiting at the pickup isn't supported by those records, and system records take priority.

Because the arrival confirmation was made away from the pickup point, your account is being sent to Ryde for review. A reviewer will look at your arrival confirmation and location records for this trip.

RydeTAXI Buona Vista MRT → Pasir Ris MRT, estimate 27.4 km / 38.0 min, pickup 18:11 drop-off 18:58; charged $59.88 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Partial refund $2.92 · account flag → a person

Issue a $2.92 Ryde voucher for the unexempted 2.07 km off-route segment, and send the driver's account for review (2 prior upheld route disputes in 90 days plus this breach) per RD-6.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 29.8 km vs optimal route 27.4 km: +2.4 km (+9%).
  • F2 Trip duration 47.9 min vs estimated 38.0 min (+9.9 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 18:37–18:46: 5.69 km driven where the optimal route covers 3.62 km, adding 2.07 km (up to 1240 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $3.47 (2.46 km × $1.4101/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F6 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F7 Total charged $59.88 SGD: base $3.90, distance $42.05 (29.82 km), time $12.35 (47.5 min), waiting $0.11, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F8 Chat #1 18:35 driver: “Jam on Pan Island Expressway is building up ahead, want me to take another way?”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The trip ran 29.8 km against an optimal 27.4 km (F1) and contains one off-route segment at 18:37–18:46 that drove 5.69 km where the optimal route covers 3.62 km, adding 2.07 km and reaching up to 1240 m from the optimal route (F3); on this RydeTAXI metered trip that extra distance flowed straight into the fare, which at this trip's per-km rate is $3.47 for 2.46 km of excess distance (F4). This segment is an unexempted deviation under RD-1 and the rider is entitled to a partial refund of the metered excess under RD-2.
  • The only rider message before the segment is '随便你，哪条快走哪条，我赶时间' at 18:35 (F9), sent after the driver's 18:35 question about the PIE jam (F8); that is at most a general 'take whichever is faster' authorisation for the route to the destination and names no road, so under RD-5 it cannot exempt a specific detour that added 2.07 km and strayed up to 1240 m from the optimal route (F3). The 18:37 segment start (F3) shows the message came before it, but the message's content does not reach that segment.
Driver's advocate
  • The complaint concerns RydeTAXI, so the fare is metered and only the metered units for any non-exempt off-route km can be refunded; there is exactly one off-route segment on this trip, F3 (18:37–18:46, 5.69 km driven vs 3.62 km optimal, +2.07 km).
  • The rider's own message sent before the segment started, F9 at 18:35 ('随便你，哪条快走哪条，我赶时间'), is a general route authorisation covering the driver's choice of the fastest way to the destination, and it precedes the F3 segment's 18:37 start, so the segment is exempt under RD-5.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 18:37:05 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F8: Chat #1 18:35 driver: “Jam on Pan Island Expressway is building up ahead, want me to take another way?”; F9: Chat #2 18:35 rider: “随便你，哪条快走哪条，我赶时间。”; F11: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “Traffic was building up ahead. I asked the rider and the rider said any faster way was fine, so I went round.” (F8, F9, F11)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F8, F9)
  • ✓ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 2 (F18)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4101/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: only if a segment is found non-exempt (RD-6 prior upheld dispute on file)
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance. The account flag is not cleared: it goes to a person in the trust and safety queue.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so $2.92 is refunded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.

No refund · account flag → a person

Close the fare dispute with no refund (fixed-fare RydeX, billed distance equals the optimal route) but record the RD-1 shortfall on the file and send the driver's account to Ryde account review for the repeated route deviation.

What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • There is one off-route segment: 09:49–09:56, where the driver covered 4.87 km against 3.00 km on the optimal route, adding 1.87 km and straying up to 1064 m from the optimal route (F3); the trip as a whole ran 19.4 km vs the 17.0 km optimal route, +2.4 km (F1), and 32.3 min vs 25.5 min estimated (F2). Under RD-1 the driver owed the shortest and most direct route absent a reasonable excuse, so this segment stands as a route deviation unless an exemption applies.
  • The only traffic record on the replaced optimal route is F11: the Pan Island Expressway at speed band 2 (10–19 km/h) during 09:23–09:49 - band 2 alone is expressly not sufficient to exempt a segment (RD-4(b), G3), and F14 records no traffic incident on the route during the trip window, so the RD-4(a) incident route to exemption is unavailable. F15 (fair weather, 0.0 mm rain) removes weather as a cause either.
Driver's advocate
  • The single off-route segment (09:49–09:56, adding 1.87 km, F3) was preceded by a route-change proposal and the rider's own consent: at 09:45 the driver wrote 'Jam on Pan Island Expressway is building up ahead, can I go round via Bedok Reservoir Road?' (F7) and at 09:46 the rider replied 'Sure, go ahead.' (F8) - the consent message precedes the segment start of 09:49, satisfying RD-5's 'before' test, and the chat record is complete with timestamps on every message (F9), so no G12 gap arises.
  • The exemption is nonetheless limited: the consent (F8) is only a general/route-choice authorisation to take Bedok Reservoir Road, and no fact in the table ties the driven 09:49–09:56 segment to that named road (F3 records the segment only by time and distance, with no road name); under RD-5 read with G13 the segment's exemption is therefore undetermined rather than automatic, which is a matter for the judge to read from the message wording.

Route deviation · B-0001

Detour · unexplained loop

24.5 s
Dispute statement

The app estimated 21 km; the driver turned off the route without a word and the meter read 24 km at the end. Please refund the extra distance.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Raffles Place → Changi Airport T3, estimate 21.2 km / 28.4 min, pickup 09:07 drop-off 09:43; charged $49.37 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Partial refund $4.33

Refund $4.33 to the rider as a Ryde voucher (NS-7) and record the RD-1 breach in the driver's file; no account flag.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 24.4 km vs optimal route 21.2 km: +3.2 km (+15%).
  • F2 Trip duration 36.8 min vs estimated 28.4 min (+8.4 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 09:24–09:30: 3.78 km driven where the optimal route covers 0.71 km, adding 3.07 km (up to 688 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $4.51 (3.20 km × $1.4101/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F6 Total charged $49.37 SGD: base $3.90, distance $34.42 (24.41 km), time $9.58 (36.8 min), waiting $0.00, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F7 Chat #1 09:04 rider: “Hi, I am at the Raffles Place taxi stand, with a black backpack.”
  • F8 Chat #2 09:05 driver: “2 min away, blue Toyota Prius.”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS track shows the driver drove 24.4 km against an optimal route of 21.2 km, an extra 3.2 km (+15%), and the trip took 36.8 min against a 28.4 min estimate (+8.4 min), so the deviation is real and not a rounding artefact.
  • One off-route segment is recorded: 09:24–09:30, 3.78 km driven where the optimal route covers 0.71 km, adding 3.07 km and straying up to 688 m off the optimal route; that segment is an unexempted detour under RD-1.
Driver's advocate
  • This is a RydeTAXI metered trip: the total charged was $49.37 = base $3.90 + distance $34.42 for 24.41 km + time $9.58 for 36.8 min + platform $1.47, with no waiting fee and no surge (F5, F6), so under the metered model only the distance units attributable to an unexempted off-route segment can be refunded, at this trip's own rate of $1.4101/km (F4).
  • The GPS record shows a single off-route segment, 09:24–09:30, driving 3.78 km where the optimal route covers 0.71 km and adding 3.07 km while straying up to 688 m from the optimal route (F3), and the total actual distance 24.4 km versus optimal 21.2 km is only +3.2 km (F1), which means the entire disputed excess is the one segment and no additional unexplained mileage exists outside it.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 09:24:50 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F7: Chat #1 09:04 rider: “Hi, I am at the Raffles Place taxi stand, with a black backpack.”; F8: Chat #2 09:05 driver: “2 min away, blue Toyota Prius.”; F10: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “I went where the GPS sent me; it rerouted. The fare is what the meter showed.” (F7, F8, F10)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F7, F8)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F16)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4101/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so $4.33 is refunded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.
Notices
Notice to the rider
We reviewed your fare dispute and are refunding you $4.33 as a Ryde voucher.

What decided it: on your metered taxi trip, part of the drive went well off the best route - about 3 km more than needed - and nothing excused it. Your driver's message that the GPS rerouted him doesn't count as a valid reason, and the chat shows no agreement to a route change.

No change to your account; just the voucher, which we'll apply to your account for your next ride.

Thanks for flagging it.
Notice to the driver
We reviewed the fare dispute on this metered taxi trip and are refunding the rider $4.33 as a Ryde voucher.

What decided it: part of the trip went off the best route - roughly 3 km extra - with no qualifying excuse. Weather and traffic conditions didn't support one, and the chat shows no route change agreed with the rider. The GPS rerouting claim wasn't accepted. This breach goes on your file.

No hold on your account - your record doesn't meet the threshold for further action. Take the best route on future trips.

Route deviation · B-0004

Detour · incident on the replaced stretch

26.6 s
Dispute statement

The driver left the route the app showed and the meter was higher than the estimate. I never agreed to any change.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Woodlands Civic Centre → Punggol Waterway Point, estimate 17.2 km / 23.6 min, pickup 17:28 drop-off 18:01; charged $45.52 (metered)

Proposed outcome

No refund

Close the case as no_action: the sole off-route segment is exempt under RD-4(a) because a recorded LTA Accident on the bypassed Seletar Expressway stretch (reported 17:16, before the 17:38 detour) supplies the reasonable cause; no refund and no account flag.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 22.2 km vs optimal route 17.2 km: +5.0 km (+30%).
  • F2 Trip duration 33.8 min vs estimated 23.6 min (+10.2 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 17:38–17:49: 6.56 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.64 km, adding 4.92 km (up to 1911 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $7.19 (5.10 km × $1.4099/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F6 Total charged $45.52 SGD: base $3.90, distance $31.37 (22.25 km), time $8.78 (33.8 min), waiting $0.00, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F7 Chat #1 17:23 rider: “I'm at the main entrance already, in a grey hoodie.”
  • F8 Chat #2 17:24 driver: “On my way, about 4 min, white Honda Vezel.”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS track shows one off-route segment, 17:38–17:49, in which 6.56 km were driven where the optimal route covers 1.64 km, adding 4.92 km and straying up to 1911 m from the optimal route, so the trip ran 22.2 km instead of 17.2 km (+30%).
  • The driver's written claim of an accident on Seletar Expressway cannot satisfy RD-4: the only incident on record is reported 17:16 at 0 m from the optimal route, and the speed band on that same optimal route was band 5 (40–49 km/h, i.e. flowing, not severe congestion), so no traffic fact justifies the 17:38–17:49 detour.
Driver's advocate
  • The single off-route segment (F3, 17:38–17:49, 4.92 km extra, up to 1911 m off the optimal route) is excused by a documented incident on the replaced stretch of the optimal route: F13 records an Accident on Seletar Expressway (towards TPE) after Woodlands Avenue 12 Exit, reported 17:16 - before the segment began - and located 0 m from the optimal route, i.e. on the very route the driver bypassed.
  • The incident evidence satisfies RD-4(a) on its face and is corroborated by the congestion band: F13 is an LTA DataMall-type Accident within the 300 m matching radius with `reported_at` 17:16 preceding the segment start 17:38, and F11 shows Seletar Expressway at speed band 5 (40–49 km/h) across 17:17–18:06, covering the segment window - so the detour had reasonable cause and no refund is due on it.

  • – RD-5 consent scope [F3] not needed: RD-4 already exempts this segment (no evidence)
  • – RD-3 rider-requested stop [F3] not needed: RD-4 already exempts this segment (no evidence)

  • allowed outcomes: no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4099/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. No refund is due.

  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.

We reviewed your trip and found no fare issue. The recorded route change was a detour around a logged highway accident on the road you would otherwise have taken, reported before the detour began, so it is treated as reasonable and not chargeable as extra distance. Result: no refund is due, and no change to your fare. Your account is not flagged and nothing is being deducted or added. The metered fare stands as charged, including the excess-distance line you were billed. If you think a fact here is wrong, reply and we will look again.

We reviewed the route dispute on this trip. The single off-route stretch was a detour around a logged highway accident on the road you would otherwise have taken, reported before you diverted, so the detour is treated as reasonable and no breach is recorded. Route deviation · B-0005

Detour · incident elsewhere (decoy)

25.7 s
Dispute statement

The app estimated 21 km but the driver went a different way and the meter came to 24 km. He never asked me. I want the extra distance refunded.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Tanjong Pagar Centre → Changi Airport T3, estimate 21.5 km / 28.4 min, pickup 18:35 drop-off 19:10; charged $47.97 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Partial refund $2.76

Refund $2.76 to the rider as a Ryde voucher (NS-7) for the unexempted 1.96 km off-route segment, and record the RD-1 route violation on the driver's file.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 23.6 km vs optimal route 21.5 km: +2.1 km (+10%).
  • F2 Trip duration 35.7 min vs estimated 28.4 min (+7.3 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 18:46–18:52: 3.50 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.54 km, adding 1.96 km (up to 752 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $3.06 (2.17 km × $1.4099/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F6 Total charged $47.97 SGD: base $3.90, distance $33.33 (23.64 km), time $9.27 (35.7 min), waiting $0.00, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F7 No in-app chat messages between rider and driver.
  • F8 Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “There was a breakdown on the route, the whole road was slow, so I took another way. Check the LTA incident feed.”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS track shows one off-route segment on 18:46–18:52, in which the driver covered 3.50 km where the optimal route covers 1.54 km, adding 1.96 km and straying up to 752 m from the optimal route - an unexplained deviation from the shortest and most direct route.
  • This is a RydeTAXI trip billed by the meter (total $47.97 with a distance component of $33.33 for 23.64 km at $1.4099/km), so the extra 1.96 km of the unexempted segment went straight into the fare, and RD-2's metered branch applies.
Driver's advocate
  • The single off-route segment ran 18:46–18:52, adding 1.96 km (F3); the driver's reason for it was a vehicle breakdown, and the LTA incident feed shows a vehicle breakdown reported at 18:27 on East Coast Parkway (towards Changi), 0 m from the optimal route - inside the 300 m matching radius and reported before the segment began (F11, F9, F10).
  • Because the breakdown report in F11 sits on the replaced section of the optimal route and predates the 18:46 segment start while the trip window ran 18:35–19:10, the segment qualifies for the RD-4(a) exemption and no refund attaches to it.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (F11)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 18:46:55 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F8: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “There was a breakdown on the route, the whole road was slow, so I took another way. Check the LTA incident feed.” (F8)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (no evidence)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F14)
  • ?   • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4099/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False

  • F7 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F8 Total charged $40.74 SGD: base $3.90, distance $26.72 (18.95 km), time $7.50 (28.8 min), waiting $1.15, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The trip is metered RydeTAXI, so extra mileage is charged to the rider: the fare bills 18.95 km of distance for a route whose optimal length is 15.5 km, and the actual GPS distance was 19.0 km, +3.5 km (+22%) over optimal (F1, F8).
  • A single off-route segment is recorded from 12:25 to 12:36: 4.57 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.60 km, adding 2.97 km and straying up to 729 m from the optimal route (F4); this segment was not exempted by any clause and is billable extra mileage under RD-1/RD-2.
Driver's advocate
  • The rider herself asked for the stop: at 12:21 she wrote "Can you stop at Paya Lebar Square? I need to grab a parcel, 3-4 minutes." (F9) and the driver accepted with "Ok can" (F10); the driver's account that he drove her there and waited is consistent (F12). This is a passenger-named stop under RD-3(a) - she named the location, Paya Lebar Square - so the driving to that stop does not by itself constitute a deviation.
  • The recorded detour is small and short-lived: one off-route segment 12:25–12:36 added 2.97 km against an optimal 1.60 km for the same stretch (F4), and the whole trip ran 19.0 km vs 15.5 km optimal (F1). The complaint's "detour overcharged" theory therefore concerns at most this single segment, not the entire fare.

Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F9: Chat #1 12:21 rider: “Can you stop at Paya Lebar Square? I need to grab a parcel, 3-4 minutes.”; F10: Chat #2 12:21 driver: “Ok can”; F12: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “The rider asked me to stop at Paya Lebar Square, so I drove there and waited while the rider went in.” (F9, F10, F12)

A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (no evidence)
  • ✓ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 2 (F14)
  • ?   • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4097/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: only if a segment is found non-exempt (RD-6 prior upheld dispute on file)

  • F1 Driver's closest approach to pickup: 599 m at 12:06:55. Driver never came within 200 m.

Route deviation · B-0049

Detour · pickup chat contains a threat hint word (ordinary message)

23.6 s
Dispute statement

The app estimated 11 km; the driver turned off the route without a word and the meter read 13 km at the end. Please refund the extra distance.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Orchard Road → NUS, estimate 10.7 km / 17.3 min, pickup 09:43 drop-off 10:03; charged $29.11 (metered)

Proposed outcome

Partial refund $2.74

Issue a $2.74 Ryde voucher (mileage unit refund for the unexempted detour segment) to the rider for trip B-0049

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 13.1 km vs optimal route 10.7 km: +2.4 km (+23%).
  • F2 Trip duration 20.2 min vs estimated 17.3 min (+2.9 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 09:47–09:53: 3.83 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.89 km, adding 1.94 km (up to 777 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $3.44 (2.44 km × $1.4101/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F6 Total charged $29.11 SGD: base $3.90, distance $18.50 (13.12 km), time $5.24 (20.2 min), waiting $0.00, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F7 Chat #1 09:35 rider: “I'll find you at the kerb outside Mandarin Gallery, I am in a red jacket.”
  • F8 Chat record: 1 message(s), every one timestamped.
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The trip was a RydeTAXI metered ride, so this is a metered-fare case where distance directly drives the fare: the total charged was $29.11 comprising base $3.90, distance $18.50 for 13.12 km, time $5.24 for 20.2 min, waiting $0.00 and platform $1.47.
  • The GPS track shows the actual distance 13.1 km against an optimal 10.7 km, i.e. +2.4 km (+23%) over an app estimate of 10.7 km / 17.3 min, and the trip took 20.2 min versus 17.3 min estimated.
Driver's advocate
  • The recorded off-route segment is 09:47–09:53, 3.83 km driven where the optimal route covers 1.89 km, adding 1.94 km (F3); the whole trip ran 13.1 km against a 10.7 km optimal route (F1). These are the only segment-level figures in the record, so any distance adjustment can only be built on the 1.94 km in F3 - the further ~0.5 km difference between the +2.4 km total (F1) and the segment sum is corridor wobble/matching noise within the 200 m corridor, which is not refundable under RD-2.
  • The single off-route segment ran 09:47–09:53, which sits inside the window 09:29–10:08 in which the LTA speed band for AYER RAJAH EXPRESSWAY was band 4, 30–39 km/h (F10), with no incidents recorded near the route (F12) and fair weather, 0.0 mm rain (F13). Band 4 is a moderate-congestion record on the optimal corridor at the time the driver deviated, and it corroborates the driver's account (F9) that his GPS rerouted him to keep the trip moving.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 09:47:50 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F7: Chat #1 09:35 rider: “I'll find you at the kerb outside Mandarin Gallery, I am in a red jacket.”; F9: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “I followed my GPS; it rerouted me. Nothing wrong with the fare.” (F7, F9)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F7)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F15)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. hint word in F7 - a locator, not a finding; read it in context (F7)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4101/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. The refund is cleared for voucher issuance.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so $2.74 is refunded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.

Trip summary

RydeTAXI Woodlands Civic Centre → Kallang Wave Mall, estimate 25.0 km / 32.7 min, pickup 14:57 drop-off 15:43; charged $58.59 (metered)

Proposed outcome

No refund

Close the case with no refund - the sole off-route segment was covered by the rider's own pre-segment general route choice, and no other clause waives the fare.

Facts the code computed
  • F1 Actual distance 29.1 km vs optimal route 25.0 km: +4.1 km (+17%).
  • F2 Trip duration 46.8 min vs estimated 32.7 min (+14.1 min).
  • F3 Off-route segment 15:21–15:30: 6.32 km driven where the optimal route covers 2.71 km, adding 3.60 km (up to 1425 m from the optimal route).
  • F4 Excess distance billed $5.88 (4.17 km × $1.4100/km, this trip's distance fare ÷ distance km).
  • F5 Waiting fee billed $0.11 (0.4 min × $0.26/min).
  • F6 Surge multiplier 1.0× (no surge).
  • F7 Total charged $58.59 SGD: base $3.90, distance $41.06 (29.12 km), time $12.05 (46.3 min), waiting $0.11, platform $1.47, discount $0.00.
  • F8 Chat #1 15:17 driver: “Traffic ahead on Pan Island Expressway is building up, shall I take another way?”
What each side argued
Rider's advocate
  • The GPS record shows exactly one off-route segment, 15:21–15:30, on which 6.32 km were driven where the optimal route covers 2.71 km, adding 3.60 km and straying up to 1425 m from the optimal route (F3); the trip as a whole ran 29.1 km against a 25.0 km optimal route (+4.1 km, +17%) (F1) and 46.8 min against a 32.7 min estimate (F2). Under RD-1 this segment is a route deviation that must be justified by RD-3, RD-4 or RD-5 or it is an unexempted deviation.
  • This is a RydeTAXI metered trip (metered total $58.59, distance fare $41.06 for 29.12 km) (F7), so the extra kilometres of an unexempted deviation feed directly into the fare: the billed excess is 4.17 km at $1.4100/km = $5.88 (F4, F7). Under RD-2 the rider is entitled to a partial refund of the unexempted segment's extra_km × the trip's per-km rate.
Driver's advocate
  • This is a RydeTAXI metered trip (F7), so the only monies in dispute are the metered distance/time units actually driven and the published meter waiting unit; the rider's complaint that 'the meter ended up well above the estimate' is answered by RD-2's mileage-only refund rule.
  • The rider's own message 'Just take whichever way is faster lah, I'm late.' (F9, 15:18) is a general route authorisation, sent before the only off-route segment began at 15:21 (F3), and message timestamps and completeness are recorded (F10), so that segment is not a deviation under RD-5.
Policy trace
  • ✓ RD-1 off-route segment(s) exist [case] 1 segment(s) (F3)
  • ✗ RD-4 (a) incident on the replaced stretch, reported before the detour [F3] no qualifying incident (speed band alone does not exempt) (no evidence)
  • ? RD-5 rider agreed to this route before the detour; scope of that agreement [F3] RULE: exempt only if a RIDER message sent before 15:21:15 agrees AND the agreement covers this segment. Three kinds of agreement: (1) GENERAL route choice ('take whichever way is faster') covers a route chosen to get there, not an errand of the driver's own (a stop away from the route, a loop that reaches nothing: counts). (2) A SPECIFIC named road or area ('go round via X') covers driving on X only: the record must tie this segment to X (a stop on X, a message placing the car on X, a road name in a fact); timing alone does not, and the fact table carries no road names for the driven path - if nothing ties them, the finding is undetermined (G13); if the record places this segment somewhere else (a stop away from X, the driver's own statement naming another road or place, a stop the driver chose the location of), the agreement to X does not cover it: counts. (3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes the amount. Evidence - F8: Chat #1 15:17 driver: “Traffic ahead on Pan Island Expressway is building up, shall I take another way?”; F9: Chat #2 15:18 rider: “Just take whichever way is faster lah, I'm late.”; F11: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “Traffic was building up ahead. I asked the rider and the rider said any faster way was fine, so I went round.” (F8, F9, F11)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F8, F9)
  • ✗ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 0 (F18)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: refund_partial, no_action, escalate; amount = Σ extra_km of non-exempt segments × $1.4100/km (distance_fare ÷ distance_km), rounded to cents
  • flag_account required: False
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. No refund is due.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found the rider's own message covers this segment, so it is exempt; without that message $5.08 would be refunded.
  • Had the driver carried a prior upheld route dispute in the last 90 days, RD-6 would add an account flag.

(3) Scope unclear: undetermined. A driver's claim of agreement that appears only in his statement is not consent (G11); a complete chat record with no rider agreement before the segment: counts. This finding changes no amount (fixed fare) but decides the RD-6 flag (the driver has a prior). Evidence - F7: Chat #1 09:45 driver: “Jam on Pan Island Expressway is building up ahead, can I go round via Bedok Reservoir Road?”; F8: Chat #2 09:46 rider: “Sure, go ahead.”; F10: Driver statement (written after the trip, not part of the chat): “Traffic was building up ahead so I asked the rider before turning off via Bedok Reservoir Road and the rider agreed.” (F7, F8, F10)
  • ? RD-3 rider-requested stop; whether the rider named this place [F3] RULE (RD-3(a)): exempt if a RIDER message before the segment named a specific place and the car stopped there (the whole way there is exempt; how long the stop lasted or how far the place was does not reduce it). A stop the rider asked for without naming a place ('a 7-Eleven on the way') does not excuse the driver's choice of a far one → counts. No stop measured in this segment. (F7, F8)
  • ✗ RD-2 metered service (detour km enters the fare) [case] RydeX is a fixed fare (no evidence)
  • ✓ RD-6 prior upheld route dispute in 90 d (repeated breach → flag_account) [case] route_disputes_upheld_90d = 2 (F17)
  • ? SAFE-1 safety screen: any threat, intimidation, harassment or dangerous-driving allegation in the chat, statement or complaint [case] answer in safety_concern (none / suspected / confirmed) with safety_basis; suspected or confirmed → escalate. no hint word; read the messages anyway, a threat needs no keyword (no evidence)
  • allowed outcomes: no_action, escalate
  • flag_account required: only if a segment is found non-exempt (RD-6 prior upheld dispute on file)
Why it was cleared

All checks passed: the policy trace allows this outcome and the amount matches what the code computed. No refund is due. The account flag is not cleared: it goes to a person in the trust and safety queue.

What would change this ruling
  • F3: the judge found no rider agreement covering this segment, so the RD-1 breach is recorded; a rider message agreeing to it before it started would have exempted it.
Notices
Notice to the rider
We looked into your fare dispute and the outcome is: no refund. RydeX is a fixed-quote service, so your fare is set when you book and doesn't change if the driver takes a longer way. The amount you were billed was based on the shortest route for your trip, and every part of the charge is a standard published item, so there's nothing refundable here.

We did see the driver went off the expected route, and because this has come up more than once, the driver's account is being sent for review. Thanks for flagging it.
Notice to the driver
Your account is being sent to Ryde account review. We checked the route record for this trip and found you drove further than the shortest route, including a stretch well off the expected line for several minutes.

Because RydeX fares are fixed at booking, the rider's fare wasn't affected and no refund was issued, but the extra distance is recorded on your file. Repeated route deviations are the reason for the review, so keep to the in-app route where you can. We'll be in touch about your account.
